Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really appreciate it. Um... I hope it's okay that I'm here, I don't want to take up your time if it's just... well, if it's just me overreacting. 

I guess I've just been a little stressed out lately. We have a new operations manager at the plant where I work—I'm a quality inspector—and he's been sort of picking apart the inspection procedures I've used for years. I'm sure he means well and is just trying to do his job, but... I just can't seem to leave work at work anymore. 

I come home all wound up, and I feel awful about it, but I've been snapping at my husband a bit. Which really isn't like me at all. Then I just lie awake at night replaying all those meetings in my head. I know I shouldn't let it get to me this much, and I'm trying to handle it better... does that make sense? Is that the sort of thing you might be able to help me with?

Um... well, I really try not to let it affect things too much, I promise. I always want to do a good job. But, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest, things have slipped just a little bit lately. 

I've missed a couple of internal deadlines at the plant, which isn't like me at all. And I haven't been going to some of the meetings I'm supposed to be at... I just kind of pull back from them right now. I hope you don't think I'm a bad employee, I really am trying to get back on track with all of that.

And at home... well, my husband and I have been bickering a little more than usual. Nothing terrible! Just... arguing a bit more. And I haven't been keeping up with our normal evening routines, like making dinner or tidying up, quite the way I used to. 

I feel awful even admitting that, because I pride myself on keeping a nice home and being a good wife. Is it normal for things to slide a little bit when you're under this much pressure? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, but I really am trying my best to fix it all.

Um... no, not really. I mean, I try to keep my spirits up as much as I can. I wouldn't say I feel sad or hopeless... definitely not most of the day or anything like that.

I really don't think I worry too much about anything else, just the work stuff right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worry about work, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I try really hard to focus on other things, I promise I do. When I'm gardening or watching TV with my husband, I make a real effort to just be in the moment. \n\nBut sometimes... well, sometimes it's like my brain just keeps looping back to what the manager said about my last report, even when I'm trying not to think about it. I can usually push it away eventually, though! I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing all day. I do manage to get it under control most of the time... is that normal? I mean, I hope it doesn't sound like I'm not trying hard enough to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body because of the stress, like feeling restless, tense muscles, trouble sleeping, or getting tired more easily than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I sleep pretty well, honestly. I always try to make sure I get a good night's rest so I'm ready for my shift. \n\nI'm on my feet a lot doing inspections at the plant, so sure, sometimes I'm a little tired at the end of the day, but just... normal tired, you know? Not because of the stress. My muscles feel fine, and I definitely don't feel restless or anything. I really try to take good care of myself physically. \n\nI hope it's a good thing that I don't have any of those issues to report? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right and giving you what you need. But yeah, physically, I promise I'm holding up just fine.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your worry about work feels persistent or hard to control, even when you're not actively thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was saying before... I really do try my best to keep it under control. I don't want to sound like I'm just letting it take over, because I'm not. \n\nI guess... sometimes it does kind of sit in the back of my mind, even when I'm not actively trying to figure out what to do about the plant. But I wouldn't say it's completely out of my control, you know? I can usually snap myself out of it if I just focus really hard on whatever I'm doing, like my gardening. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just don't want you to think I'm not handling things well. I'm really trying to be positive about it all. Is it okay that it still pops up sometimes, even when I'm trying to be good and not dwell on it?\n\nDoctor: Has this stress about work made it harder for you to function in any important areas of your life, like your job performance, relationships, or daily routines?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to let it affect things too much, I promise. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no... no, I don't think so. I really pride myself on being very attentive. In my line of work, you know, as an inspector, you absolutely have to stay focused, so I always make sure that I am. Even if I have a lot on my mind with all the new plant standards, I don't let it distract me from the actual task right in front of me. 

I would hate for anyone to think I'm slipping or not doing my job properly, so I push through it. And at home... well, I just get my chores done like always. I try very hard to stay on top of everything and not let my mind wander. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers and being helpful.
